## Bounded owner placement implementation plan — 2026-10-22

Sys-init0205/BG-072 now supplies a measured requirement: native gate2968 bytes
plus fixed RPC workspace4096 cannot fit the existing4088-byte entry stack. Consume

watchdog/workload/capture limits. No dynamic supervisor allocation or new syscall.

Trusted initial registers identify the selection and region: rdi30, r10=0x900000,
r11=8192; existing factory/block/image/channel/cleanup registers stay unchanged.
Consume the two scalars at entry before ordinary calls overwrite volatile registers.
This boot mapping is the authority; a client-supplied address/ABI number is not.
The region belongs only to the boot owner for its VM lifetime, not to copied or
launched processes. No owner replacement/reuse is introduced. Old profiles map no
such region and receive no new startup binding. Pages outside the region remain
unmapped. Code tails remain immutable; no writable image statics or alias shortcuts.

Security/validation: check exact envelope/build selection before mapping; zero
each selected backing page before user exposure; use existing partition/mapping
machinery and verify disjoint code/stack/peers, RW/NX flags, boundary holes and
old-profile absence. Execute actual mapping/startup bodies with host doubles and
strictly compiling zeroing/permission/profile/boot-binding omissions. Run affected
parser/build guards and optimized x86_64 kernel compilation. Native execution,
full owner peak fit and affected-consumer acceptance remain separate pending the
matching sys-init owner; do not spend a consumed persistence campaign approval.
Record all failures, selected inputs/tools/commands and compact results; clean
owned outputs. No sibling edit/message or broad unrelated runtime refactoring.

Simplicity: one fixed boot-owned mapping is smaller and narrower than a general
allocator or another service. Shrinking the pinned4096-byte workspace is not a
current compatible alternative; a larger stack would mix resident data with call
frames and change its bound. Reuse existing backing and page mapping, retain one
FILE/stream owner, and remove the need for impossible stack/code-tail placement.
The1128-byte remainder is not proof that all future owner metadata fits: sys-init
must finish and measure its complete layout before another mapping is considered.

## What the source establishes

| Area | Pinned upstream evidence | Aether implication |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Engine entry | [libzpool build](https://github.com/openzfs/zfs/blob/71a9f9578616a90c3c14bb59629fb4d31bfd68d1/lib/libzpool/Makefile.am) lists 178 C translation-unit entries and links libicp, libunicode, libnvpair, libzstd, libzutil, zlib, math and pthread support | Start from this user-space engine route, not the Linux kernel module. Entry count is not executable size or a minimal dependency closure. |
| Threading | [kernel emulation](https://github.com/openzfs/zfs/blob/71a9f9578616a90c3c14bb59629fb4d31bfd68d1/lib/libzpool/kernel.c) and [task queues](https://github.com/openzfs/zfs/blob/71a9f9578616a90c3c14bb59629fb4d31bfd68d1/lib/libzpool/taskq.c) use thread creation/join, mutexes, condition waits and TLS; default created stack is 256 KiB | Current independently scheduled tasks do not supply shared-address-space pthread semantics. Lock no-ops or silently synchronous task queues would change required behavior. |
| Memory | [ARC limits](https://github.com/openzfs/zfs/blob/71a9f9578616a90c3c14bb59629fb4d31bfd68d1/module/zfs/arc.c#L7980) set a default minimum floor of 32 MiB | Current native backing ceiling is 128 pages (512 KiB), including image/stack and anonymous pages. Raising only the 1-MiB ELF file-size limit is insufficient. The ARC floor is not total RSS or a proven minimum viable port budget. |
| Device size | [SPA constants](https://github.com/openzfs/zfs/blob/71a9f9578616a90c3c14bb59629fb4d31bfd68d1/include/sys/fs/zfs.h#L990) define a 64-MiB minimum device | The 8-MiB delegated fixture is too small for the unchanged engine. Use a larger disposable volume for engine tests; the region ABI itself does not hardcode 8 MiB. |
| Device callbacks | [vdev file I/O](https://github.com/openzfs/zfs/blob/71a9f9578616a90c3c14bb59629fb4d31bfd68d1/module/zfs/vdev_file.c) borrows ABD buffers, submits work, reports residual bytes and issues fsync completion | Add a private grant-bound vdev adapter. Byte offsets map to the existing region; chunking larger I/O must preserve completion/error semantics. Native 4-KiB wrapper calls are not filesystem transaction boundaries. |
| Platform assumptions | kernel.c uses allocation, clocks, sysconf, host-file operations and random-device input | Replace the actually used platform boundary with explicit resource/clock/entropy/device authority. Names or UID 0 cannot open arbitrary Aether devices. Existing fixed startup random bytes are not a runtime entropy service. |
| Memory-pressure shim | [libzpool ARC platform code](https://github.com/openzfs/zfs/blob/71a9f9578616a90c3c14bb59629fb4d31bfd68d1/lib/libzpool/arc_os.c) uses randomized pressure/free-memory behavior | It is not a production quota policy for a capability-scoped volume service. Supply measured, bounded accounting instead of transplanting the testing shim unchanged. |
| Filesystem interface | libzpool's source list contains DMU/DSL/ZAP, but omits platform zfs_vnops and zfs_dir.c | Pool/object success is not create/lookup/rename/unlink/path semantics. A filesystem namespace layer still needs porting and qualification; do not invent a replacement on-disk filesystem around DMU objects. |

Local enforcement sources are [ELF validation](../kernel/native_elf.zig),
[native task backing/dispatch](../kernel/arch/x86_64/native_c.zig),
[memory accounting](../kernel/native_memory.zig) and
[Rust block transport](../user/storage/block.rs). An arena address range is not
additional physical backing. Clone/futex/general pthread runtime support is not
implemented by the current dispatch. Existing bounded init deadline observations
must not be represented as the complete clock/wait interface required here.

The user-configure checks in `config/user.m4` cover broader host utilities as well
as libraries. Do not classify every optional host package probe as a required
Aether engine dependency; measure the selected static link closure first.

## Recommended executable sequence

1. **Supported-host engine baseline.** Pin a Linux build environment and exact
   configuration for this source. In a private output directory, build the selected
   user-space engine and run a disposable single-vdev workload that creates,
   writes, syncs, exports and imports retained data. Start with a 256-MiB disposable
   image as an experiment size, not a proven minimum. Record link dependencies,
   undefined platform symbols, startup/peak memory, live threads and stack bounds.
   Host success is explicitly not Aether execution or full filesystem acceptance.
2. **Measured native runtime contract.** Use those measurements to define a
   capability-scoped task memory budget, loader bounds, shared-address-space
   threads/TLS, waits and time/entropy sources. Aether owns mechanisms; the service
   owns cache/work-queue policy. Test exhaustion and teardown before making the
   engine depend on them. Do not just increase global fixed arrays for every task.
3. **Native engine I/O.** Port the private platform/vdev boundary and connect one
   writer to one delegated volume through Rust. Start with a declared narrow
   feature set and preserve unsupported-feature rejection. Specify C buffer,
   allocation, callback, concurrency and unwind contracts before implementation.
   Demonstrate pool import and data verification, then writes/flush/export/import.
4. **Real filesystem and release composition.** Port the required namespace/file
   operations, expose directory-relative capability access, and integrate the
   consumer-selected shell/runner/package workload through sys-init. Qualify full
   storage, interruption, corrupted metadata and recovery on disposable images.
   Snapshot/rollback acceptance is explicit and separate from package/service
   activation, not inferred from OpenZFS's feature list.

Steps 1–3 now have bounded executable evidence in STATE. Next is step 4: the
Rust directory-relative capability boundary over the qualified read-only adapter,
followed by genuine upstream namespace mutations and consumer composition. This plan
retains OpenZFS as the leading evaluation direction without claiming its port is
cheap or guaranteed. If the measured runtime/port scope is unacceptable for R1,
review an alternative mature engine with the maintainer before changing direction;
do not silently choose a new filesystem or discard snapshots as a long-term goal.

## Read-only ZPL adapter contract — 2026-10-18

Requirement before implementation: begin real filesystem access using upstream
ZPL master-node, directory ZAP and registered SA attributes, rather than promoting
the existing `DMU_OST_OTHER` fixture into a filesystem. Batch root validation,
single-component lookup and bounded regular-file reads in a private C adapter.
This is a prerequisite for the Rust service, not a shared protocol or consumer
handoff. Aether owns this adapter; userland retains POSIX translation, sys-init
supervision, and pkg-mgr installation/publication policy. Current sibling STATE,
userland NATIVE-DASH-GAPS and pkg-mgr ISO-PACKAGE-TEST were read; no sibling changes.

The trusted engine owner supplies a live READ-only SPA borrow and its configured
dataset name. The adapter exclusively owns that `DMU_OST_ZFS` objset until close;
it must disown before SPA export/retirement. Calls are serialized with no concurrent
dataset mutation, close, import/export or engine teardown. Dataset names and raw
object/generation pairs are **private owner inputs, not capability handles**.
The later Rust service must bind opaque handles to delegated connections, rights
and service generations before exposing these operations. No ambient lookup,
caller-selected pool, public object-number access or UID-based authority is added.

Allowed format: unencrypted ZPL5, SA inodes, case-sensitive names, normalization0
and utf8only0. Require a genuine master node, SA registration object and a linked
directory root whose parent is itself. Reject unsupported properties and inode
types rather than inventing translations. Lookup accepts one byte component of
1–255 bytes, excluding NUL, slash, `.` and `..`; there is no symlink traversal,
mount crossing, `.zfs` synthesis or parent lookup. Check actual object type and
generation on every directory/read use; directory-entry type must agree with SA.
Only linked regular files and directories are returned. Single-owner/quiescent
media is essential: generation checks do not make concurrent rename safe.

Output nodes publish only on success. Reads use original `dmu_read`, explicit
offsets, a maximum4096-byte borrowed output, EOF clipping and no prefetch. Report
count only after success; clear the requested buffer on read failure so a partial
engine copy is not accidentally published. No C pointer escapes except the opaque
owned context; SA handles are private, destroyed on every acquired path, and the
objset-owned registration map is borrowed until disown. No callback, unwind,
transaction, write/ZIL, custom on-disk record, kernel/SDK/ABI or resource-ceiling
change. C remains in the data TCB; wrapping it later does not prove engine safety.

Simplicity SE-01–05: the simpler alternative is direct DMU-object access, which
cannot satisfy a real filesystem namespace. Reuse original ZAP/SA/DMU readers
and upstream format constants without porting Linux inode/page-cache or FreeBSD
vnode machinery into this read-only slice. Full create/rename/unlink/write and
recovery still need a deliberate upstream namespace port; this adapter does not
substitute for those operations. No existing experimental path is removed now.

Test selection: execute the actual adapter with fault-injecting host API doubles,
checking root/format/type/generation/name denials, exact lookup/read results,
zero/short/EOF/overflow reads, failure-output clearing and balanced ownership.
Compiling omission controls must fail the named semantic oracle. Compile the full
adapter against pinned native OpenZFS/musl headers and inspect imports for writes
or ambient host I/O. This does **not** establish real SA/ZAP decoding, native
execution or persistence: next require an independently legitimate ZPL namespace
fixture and read-only same-media guest qualification before service integration.
Focused checks suffice while the adapter is unselected by existing builds; the
next composed filesystem-service handoff requires the broad checkpoint. Retain
compact commands/hashes/failures only and dispose owned outputs.

Preparation result: [compact receipt](evidence/openzfs-zpl-read-preparation-2026-10-18.json)
records nine focused methods,78 actual-adapter host scenarios and seven compiling
omission controls rejected at `HOST ZPL oracle92`. It covers original-style
set-output-then-error ownership, nonidentity SA attribute registration, every
open/lookup/read API failure point, stale identities, incompatible metadata,
traversal/type rejection, short/EOF/zero/4096-byte reads and failed partial-copy
clearing. The full6160-byte x86_64 object compiles against the existing pinned
common-znode header recipe; its consumed dependency bytes match current-inputs.
Its18 direct imports are read/metadata, allocation/memory and stack-protection
operations, with no write transaction or host-file I/O. This original-header
recipe still maps allocation to libc calloc/free; the active resource-aware
allocator binding and final engine link remain **unqualified**, not silently
adopted. The adapter is not selected by any existing engine/profile build.

Earlier compile/test setup failures are retained: wrong include recipe/order,
nonexistent encryption accessor, host string-pointer warning, a mutant's unused
parameter and an incorrect allocator-import expectation. No guest was started,
and no native persistence outcome or recurrence occurred. Temporary compiler
objects/executables are disposed by class/temporary-directory cleanup. Kernel,
runtime, SDK, grants, quotas, standards pins, default profiles and siblings are

same-media qualification, then the Rust capability boundary and real upstream

## Ownership, dependencies and verification

### Rust ZPL composition contract — 2026-10-18

Before implementation: connect the private Rust namespace owner to the existing
original-C ZPL reader, then upgrade only the opt-in `--pool-namespace` experiment
to execute that composition. The old C-only consumer stays a host regression
reference. No new selector, public wire API, endpoint, SDK, kernel primitive,
ambient attach, credential policy, mutation or companion handoff. Defaults and
existing native limits/pins remain unchanged. Aether owns the adapter and enforcing
owner; userland/sys-init/pkg-mgr policies keep their current owners.

The private C importer borrows a live readonly SPA for one synchronous Rust probe.
Only that probe can construct the Rust ZPL backend. It opens the fixed dataset,
owns the returned C context and consumes it once through Drop before returning to
C. SPA remains borrowed through every Rust owner/context Drop; no pointer escapes,
concurrent import/export/replacement, callback into the owner, unwinding through
C or cross-thread backend transfer. The Rust backend is neither Clone nor Send.
C's node layout is four u64 fields, verified on both sides. Zero/nonpositive status
and returned context/metadata/count combinations are checked explicitly: positive
errno is preserved, negative status/zero-success-null/impossible completion reject.
Any nonnull open output is owned and valid for close, including error outputs;
original C currently nulls them on failure. C calls are trusted implementation,
not a parser for arbitrary client pointers. Valid nonaliasing pointers/slices stay
borrowed through each call; failed reads clear requested output.

The probe uses fixed internal epochs and peer labels solely to exercise owner
checks within one fresh private engine. They are not authenticated IPC evidence or
production provisioning. Lookup/read goes through Rust handles and the C backend.
Require cross-peer/session/right denials, traversal/missing/type checks, stale
closed/reused handles, disconnect revocation and a fresh epoch rejecting old tokens.
Read the complete780248-byte file through the owner, preserving exact EOF-clipped
completion and zeroed tails. A fixed C hash-update helper consumes only returned
bytes into the caller's stack-owned original SHA2 context; no function-pointer
callback or new hash implementation. That context stays valid through the probe
and is never retained. C finalization must match the independent reference SHA256.
Owner shutdown and the restarted owner must both close their contexts before
return; only then may original queued-user drain/readonly SPA retirement and the

### Rust namespace owner contract — 2026-10-18

Before implementation: add a private, no_std Rust owner core for the qualified
read-only ZPL adapter. Aether owns native filesystem enforcement; Aether-userland
keeps POSIX descriptor/path translation, sys-init keeps lifecycle policy and
pkg-mgr keeps installation policy. Current sibling contracts offer no persistent
namespace wire protocol to adopt. This slice defines no shared ABI, service
publication, endpoint, kernel primitive, public SDK or consumer acceptance.
A native C-context/transport binding must be qualified before publication.

One exclusive Rust owner owns one backend and all mutable connection/handle state.
Use four connections and sixteen total handles for this experiment, with no heap,
locks, hidden globals or per-operation allocation. These are private table bounds,
not new kernel quotas. The backend owns the dataset context and its final Drop;
shutdown first revokes all handles/connections, then drops it once. Operations are
synchronous exclusive borrows: no pending backend call, retained request slice,
callback, concurrent shutdown or automatic replay is permitted in this first core.

Trusted provisioning supplies a nonzero service-instance epoch that must never be
reused at the same enforcing endpoint, and explicitly attaches an authenticated
nonzero peer binding with a root-rights ceiling. Peer bindings come from retained
kernel/transport authority, never request fields or POSIX IDs. Attach is an owner
administration operation, not a client opcode. Each request supplies that trusted
binding separately from its opaque session/handle tokens. Both tokens carry the
service epoch and a monotonically issued serial; the owner checks peer, session,
handle owner, epoch, state and rights before backend access. Tokens are meaningful
only at the issuing owner, not globally valid capabilities. Serial exhaustion
fails closed without wrap/reuse; reconnect/slot reuse cannot revive old handles.
The future transport must enforce this provenance, not deserialize a claimed peer.

Operations: trusted attach returns one root directory handle; lookup is relative
to an owned directory and requests an explicit nonzero subset of its LOOKUP/READ
ceiling; read requires READ and a regular file; close consumes one handle;
disconnect consumes its session and every associated handle; shutdown consumes the
backend. Lookup names are 1–255 bytes with no slash, NUL, dot or dot-dot. No path
resolution, symlinks, delegation/duplication, cursor, POSIX credential policy,
mutation or generic VFS is added. Reply metadata contains kind/size only, never
ZFS object/generation identity. Those values remain private backend inputs.
Read accepts one fixed 4096-byte reply buffer and a bounded requested length,
checks offset overflow, requires the exact EOF-clipped completion, clears the
whole reply on error and zeros the unused successful tail. Partial backend writes
or impossible counts cannot publish data. Full tables and exhausted serials deny
before backend effects; rejected lookup cannot leak a table slot or serial.

Security requirements: cross-peer/session/owner, stale close/reconnect/restart,
rights escalation and malformed names must reject before backend calls. Root and
lookup metadata are validated before handle publication. Backend root admission
failure drops its owned context. Closing one handle does not close the shared
backend or revoke unrelated handles; shutdown is explicit and terminal. A test
backend is trusted provider code, not evidence of authenticated IPC or real ZPL.
No credential authorization beyond explicit delegated scope is claimed; it must
be reviewed before exposing newly acquired access to broader callers.

Simplicity SE-01–05: a direct fixed table with one narrow backend trait is chosen
because the current Rust owner must enforce the same contract over the existing
C adapter and failure-injectable tests. Raw object numbers/client-side masks would
be simpler but bypass the required authority boundary. A general VFS, async worker
layer, new transport or POSIX descriptor broker adds unneeded state. None of the
still-consumed probes can be removed now; retain them for native comparison.

Focused acceptance: actual Rust owner tests for valid access/composition,
peer/session/rights/type/name denials, stale reuse/restart, table/serial exhaustion,
partial backend failure/impossible completion, and exact context-drop behavior.
Compiling broken guards must fail named semantic assertions. Compile optimized
x86_64-unknown-none code and assert the table layout budget under pinned Rust1.98.0.
Keep compact source/tool/command/failure results and dispose owned outputs. Reuse
unchanged C/native/default evidence only for its original scope. No QEMU/full-host
repeat for this unselected private core; the next original-C/native service
composition needs actual target provenance and the broad integration checkpoint.

Command: `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_namespace_owner.py -v`.
[Compact owner receipt](evidence/namespace-owner-2026-10-18.json) records three
passing methods: ten actual Rust tests, eight separately compiling omitted guards
that fail named assertions, and optimized panic-abort x86_64-unknown-none codegen.
The probe's fixed owner storage is 1,048 bytes, aligned8, with16-byte session and
handle tokens, within the 2,048-byte private table budget. Its 4,896-byte object is
unlinked; it uses a synthetic provider, not the original engine or a native guest.
Rust1.98.0 warnings/format, workflow and simplicity checks are recorded. Temporary
outputs are disposed; no guest, full-suite repeat, SDK/ABI/pin/limit/sibling change.

Implementation: `user/storage/namespace.rs` owns all tables and checks; replies
contain no object/generation identity. `tests/fixtures/namespace_owner.rs` supplies
faultable host-only state, while `namespace_owner_target.rs` instantiates native
code and emits the inspectable layout. Neither fixture is a service or an access
route. The trait is a private trusted provider seam, not a published extensible VFS.
The unchanged original-C/native result cannot qualify this new Rust boundary.

Next compose an owned C ZPL context under the live SPA borrow, convert positive
errno/metadata/completion explicitly, and ensure Rust owner shutdown/drop closes
the context before original SPA drain/retirement. Qualify that exact native link
and execution before wiring authenticated transport. Trusted provisioning must
supply a never-reused owner epoch; transport must project the actual peer binding
separately from untrusted request bytes. No ambient attach operation, raw object

## Supported-host experiment contract — 2026-09-06

Build the retained commit in a private Linux/arm64 container (the available native
host architecture), then exercise upstream user-space engine tools on newly created
regular-file vdevs only. This establishes a host baseline, not x86_64 code size,
Aether compatibility, namespace service acceptance or a production memory budget.
Prepare tools through an explicit network-enabled image build; record package
versions and freeze the resulting image ID before offline upstream compilation.
The setup recipe is not a reproducible package repository snapshot.

No kernel module, /dev/zfs, privileged container, host device passthrough or system
installation. Drop capabilities; no network during source builds/workloads. Bound
container memory/PIDs and command duration. Retain source/input digests, configure/
compiler output, ELF dependencies and every attempted workload. A successful
upstream stress-tool exit is not automatically export/import persistence evidence;
only claim phases and data checks actually observed. Rust/native sources and
shared contracts are unchanged. Preselected checks: engine build/link, disposable
workload, resource observation, source integrity, host/standards and documentation.

## Supported-host result — 2026-09-06

The retained source builds `libzpool.la`, `ztest` and `zdb` on Linux/arm64 with
GCC 14.2.0 in pinned tool image
`ef2164012fa1c3a315d34091c6ed8951d3eac37c7192d5adbb5bf4c1a30d916f`.
Configuration: `--with-config=user --disable-shared --enable-static --disable-pyzfs`;
run `make gitrev` before the selected targets. No original source file changed.
OpenZFS libraries are static here, but the resulting host tools still dynamically
link libc and other system libraries; this is not a freestanding static Aether ELF.

A 256-MiB file-vdev workload passes with one selected test worker, one dataset,
no mirrored copies, no injected kills and special vdevs disabled. The tool's nominal
three-second workload takes 30.47 seconds including setup/checks. A new container's
read-only `zdb` import/checksum traversal passes with no space-map leaks, and the
whole disk SHA-256 is unchanged. Invalid all-zero media rejects all four labels.
This is real upstream engine execution and retained-data verification, not an
Aether filesystem or a fixed application-file/namespace workload.

| Observation | Exact scope |
| --- | --- |
| GNU time maximum RSS: 215248 KiB (about 210 MiB) | Successful create/test invocation and its child resource accounting; not an engine-only minimum |
| Sampled combined RSS peak: 218644 KiB | Sum of live ztest/zdb process RSS at 20-ms polling; shared pages may be double counted and short peaks missed |
| Sampled threads: 257 during create, 227 during separate verification | Combined ztest/zdb threads, not just the single requested workload worker |
| ztest text/data/BSS: 3317823 / 128104 / 1310648 bytes | Linux/arm64 GCC build; excludes dynamic libraries, heaps and stacks; not an x86_64 estimate |
| zdb text/data/BSS: 3384624 / 128968 / 1311136 bytes | Same limitations |

The separate **writable `ztest -E` reopen does not pass**. Discovery calls the
Linux `libzpool_config_ops.pool_active` helper, which opens `/dev/zfs` and returns
an error when the device is absent. The subsequent no-pools assertion is retained.
`zdb` supports an explicit read-only active-pool inspection path and passes without
that device. We did not load a kernel module or replace the active-pool check with
an unconditional inactive answer. The next host experiment needs an explicitly
owned-file pool/import adapter or a source-reviewed harness adjustment with real
exclusion, deterministic data and export/import acceptance; it must not become a
general active-pool safety bypass.

[All attempts and artifacts](evidence/openzfs-host-2026-09-06/README.md) retain
setup/build failures, the rejected documented `-C off` option (this source requires
`-C special=off`), the writable reopen failure, final read-only success, image/package
identity, build products, dependency/symbol listings and measurement logs. There is
no measured live-thread stack high-water mark, repeated performance baseline,
x86_64 build or complete platform-symbol closure yet. Archive `nm -u` output contains
inter-object references too, so it is not by itself a list of native runtime imports.

Next: close controlled writable export/import with deterministic retained data,
then build/measure x86_64 and separate harness overhead from engine requirements
before selecting native memory/thread budgets. Current 512-KiB task backing cannot
host this built workload; no numerical replacement budget is adopted from one run.

## Owned-file writable experiment contract — 2026-09-06

Before implementation: create one new O_EXCL/O_NOFOLLOW regular-file vdev in a
private container workspace; hold its descriptor and exclusive advisory lock
through all engine lifetimes. Reject an existing path without mutation and verify
that an independent open cannot acquire the held lock. This protects cooperating
fixture processes only; private workspace ownership is essential and it is not a
replacement for general active-pool or kernel device exclusion.

Use only the in-memory configuration returned by this fixture's spa_export.
Never load caller-supplied configuration or scan directories for arbitrary pools.
Create a DMU object with deterministic 4096-byte contents, sync/export, shut down
the engine, initialize it again, import writable and verify. Write a second pattern,
sync/export and repeat initialization/import/verification. This is DMU persistence,
not a new on-disk format, POSIX namespace or filesystem service. Keep upstream
sources and active-pool checks unchanged. A wrong expected byte must reject at the
payload check, not a generic startup failure. Retain exact source/build hashes and
all attempts; host/standards/format checks apply, native QEMU does not.

New original C fixture is BSD-2-Clause, links existing pinned host libraries and
uses their documented borrowed object/transaction/config ownership. Release
objset holds before exporting; retain no engine pointers across kernel_fini; pack
configuration into ordinary host-owned memory before shutdown and unpack after
initialization. Abort on unexpected engine errors; no recovery/retry claim. The
container watchdog bounds a hang and preserves its disposable files. No new
native ABI, dependency, shared standard, consumer pin or service policy.

## Owned-file result — 2026-09-06

[The Linux-only fixture](../user/storage/host-owned.c) now passes two controlled
writable export/import cycles through libzpool. It creates a fresh private file,
holds its exclusive advisory lock, creates one DMU object, writes 4096 deterministic
bytes, waits for sync and exports. It packs only the returned configuration,
fully shuts down/reinitializes the engine, imports writable and verifies every
byte. A second pattern repeats that sequence, then final export completes.
No discovery callback is replaced; the earlier generic ztest -E failure remains
unchanged. No caller-supplied pool/configuration is accepted.

The final [qualification](evidence/openzfs-owned-2026-09-06/README.md) passes:

- Two writable imports and exact payload checks, with duplicate-import rejection.
- An independent descriptor cannot acquire the held advisory lock.
- Reusing the existing file path rejects before any mutation; full disk hash agrees.
- Compiling a wrong expected byte fails specifically at `payload mismatch` after
  the first import. It is retained as an intentional failure on separate media.
- A fresh container with the positive disk mounted read-only passes zdb checksum
  traversal with no space-map leaks; the complete disk hash remains unchanged.
- Frozen source/binary identity, host/standards/workflow and scoped C format checks.

Final GNU time maximum RSS is 23248 KiB (about 23 MiB) for this Linux/arm64 workload;
initial success measured 23472 KiB. These are small-workload observations, not cache
reservations, a minimum/maximum filesystem budget or full service measurements.
The writable engine reinitializations occur within one process; the separate
read-only verifier is a fresh process/container. No fresh-process writable import,
power-loss, interrupted export recovery or noncooperating-writer exclusion claim.
Fatal assertion paths preserve the disposable image and rely on process/container
exit, not a production service cleanup/recovery protocol.

Next: build this exact fixture and engine for x86_64, then measure the selected
runtime requirements before defining native memory/thread contracts. The successful
DMU object is not a home-grown filesystem: the actual namespace layer, Rust service
interface and shell/package composition remain required and unimplemented.

## x86_64 baseline result — 2026-09-06

The same pinned OpenZFS 2.4.4 source and owned-file C fixture cross-build with
GCC 14.2.0 for Linux/x86_64. All original upstream source files compare unchanged.
The [retained qualification](evidence/openzfs-x86-2026-09-06/README.md) passes two
writable export/import payload checks, the existing-file/no-mutation control,
intentional wrong-byte rejection and a fresh read-only zdb checksum/space-map
traversal. Host/standards/workflow, format and frozen-input checks pass.

The engine libraries are statically linked, but the executable is a Linux PIE
with an interpreter and dynamic system-library dependencies. Its on-disk size is
18,608,720 bytes including debug/nonloaded material. Four PT_LOAD segments occupy
1,287 pages, or 5,271,552 bytes, before any shared libraries, heaps or stacks.
Aether currently accepts bounded static ET_EXEC images, with a 1-MiB file limit
and 128 total image/stack/backing pages (512 KiB). Stripping debug or raising only
the file limit cannot bridge these separate runtime and mapped-memory gaps.
No dynamic-loader or wholesale Linux syscall compatibility decision follows.

Execution uses QEMU 10.0.11 user emulation with explicit qemu64 CPU and target
Linux loader/libraries. The positive run's 2.60-second elapsed time and 74,728-KiB
maximum RSS include emulation overhead. They are not native performance or a
selected service-memory budget. A separate passing guest trace observes mmap,
mprotect, brk, clone, futex, clocks, entropy, pread64/pwrite64 and fsync, among
other calls. Counts are line-leading observations from interleaved output, not

## Runtime reassessment after paired condition waits — 2026-09-06

This is the source-review baseline. Subsequent runtime witnesses and the current
next action are recorded in [STATE](../.planning/STATE.md); the table's unqualified
items describe the point of review, not a second current backlog. In particular,
fixture-level pthread evidence does not qualify OpenZFS's default worker counts,
wall-clock calls or full engine allocation behavior.

The earlier 512-KiB/1-MiB limits above describe that baseline, not today's opt-in
profile. Current native experiments have an 8-MiB backing profile, larger static
image admission, two live child slots and four lifetime child admissions. Actual
musl create/join, mutex contention, paired condition signal/broadcast/requeue and
supervised cleanup now pass. They still do not provide the full libzpool runtime.

[Source review receipt](evidence/libzpool-runtime-review-2026-09-06/README.md)
binds the unchanged OpenZFS 2.4.4 source to the original archive. The following
requirements come from the user-space engine path, not an undifferentiated list
of Linux syscalls observed under QEMU:

| Requirement / source | Current disposition and required work |
| --- | --- |
| `kernel_init` calls `system_taskq_init`; `taskq.c` requests 64 system and four delayed-task workers | Two live children/four admissions cannot start these defaults. Select and measure a service task-queue policy before defining scalable owner-local storage. Do not silently serialize tasks or inflate all static domain arrays. |
| `zk_thread_create` defaults to detached unless TS_JOINABLE; taskq creation supplies TS_RUN | The fixture accepts joinable children only. Detached completion must safely retire after leaving the child stack/TLS; join substitution is not equivalent. Define explicit completion/reaping ownership before adding this path. |
| Each default taskq worker requests TS_STACK_MAX = 256 KiB with a page guard | 68 × 256 KiB = 17 MiB of requested stacks alone, excluding guards, TLS, image and heaps. This is source-derived demand, not measured resident usage or a recommended budget. It exceeds the current 8-MiB total backing profile. |
| `rw_init/enter/tryenter/exit` wrap pthread rwlocks | Native reader/writer contention is unqualified. Next bounded witness can use existing private wait/wake operations; no new kernel rwlock object is justified. |
| `cv_timedwait`/`cv_timedwait_hires` use gettimeofday/gethrtime and pthread_cond_timedwait | Current condition witnesses are untimed. A bounded tick wait is not a POSIX clock. Define clock identity, units, absolute-deadline conversion, overflow and timeout-vs-wake semantics before adapting timed waits. |
| taskq pthread keys and recursive-rwlock TSD with destructor | Compiler TLS evidence does not establish pthread key/destructor lifecycle. Qualify actual key isolation and destruction, including detached cleanup. |
| `kernel_init`, allocation/cache sizing and x86 baseline ELF | The retained executable is Linux PIE with dynamic system libraries, not an Aether ET_EXEC image. A static musl/platform rebuild and measured allocation/stack/cache behavior are still required; mapped ELF pages or emulator RSS are insufficient budgets. |
| Engine file/vdev paths use open/pread/pwrite/fsync, host discovery and entropy | Port the C platform boundary behind a Rust-owned delegated volume. No ambient host path access, fake flush, fixed entropy or imported Linux authority model. Existing block primitives are prerequisites, not a mounted filesystem. |

The existing `undefined.txt` lists archive-member imports, including references
resolved by other members. The new dynamic-symbol receipt describes imports of
the retained Linux executable, including host/loader dependencies. Neither list
is a complete minimal native ABI or evidence that every symbol is executed.

### Next executable witness: actual musl read/write locks

Use the current paired lifecycle fixture and unchanged capability grants. One
batch holds read locks in both children while the parent tries then waits for a
write lock; a second holds the parent writer while both readers must block.
Prove simultaneous readers, exclusive writer, actual wait/wake, failed trylock
without mutation, unlock progress, joins, empty lock/wait state and restored quota
across batches and supervisor replacement. Use a semantic bypass control that
fails the named exclusion assertion rather than merely timing out. Preserve the
four-admission limit, existing masks/TLS and both memory-profile regressions.

After that witness: key/destructor lifetime, explicit time/deadline contracts,
then detached completion and measured taskq/allocator budgets before full engine
startup. These are required port work, not a promise that one more primitive makes
libzpool bootable. Retain the mature-engine direction and Rust service ownership;
no engine replacement, native ABI, policy or consumer pin is selected here.

## Named worker attribution and service-policy boundary — 2026-09-06

The [attribution receipt](evidence/openzfs-taskq-attribution-2026-09-06/README.md)
adds an opt-in name observer. Ten fresh processes after a warmup reproduce the
102 first-init and 740 total creation requests. All 49 final name groups reconcile
to the create count; group counts are identical across the ten samples. Names
are requested labels, not live thread IDs or unique taskq identities. In particular,
`arc_evict` combines an eight-worker taskq with one zthr coordinator.

| First-init group | Requests | Source of the request |
| --- | --- | --- |
| system_taskq / delay_taskq | 64 / 4 | lib/libzpool/taskq.c system_taskq_init; fixed constants |
| arc_prune | 1 | module/zfs/arc.c; static prune-thread default |
| arc_evict | 9 | Eight workers from the max_ncpus=64 eviction formula, plus the same-named zthr; this is not a nine-worker taskq |
| arc_flush | 4 | MIN(boot_ncpus, 4) |
| arc_reap | 1 | Separate zthr coordinator |
| dbu_evict / dbuf_evict_thread | 1 / 1 | Taskq and separate dbuf eviction thread |
| z_vdev_file | 16 | module/zfs/vdev_file.c uses MAX(boot_ncpus, 16) |
| l2arc_feed_thread | 1 | Separate L2ARC feed thread |

Three initializations account for 306 of 740 lifetime requests. Pool and transient
work account for 434 more, including 216 ZIO issue/completion workers. The full
49-group data remains in attribution.json; this classification is not a live
census or proof that differently named queues can share an executor.

Important port constraints from unchanged sources:

- In this user-space build, boot_ncpus reads sysconf online CPUs (nine here), while
  max_ncpus is the compile-time constant 64. They are different inputs.
- libzpool taskq_create eagerly creates nthreads. TASKQ_DYNAMIC does not make this
  implementation lazily allocate workers. taskq_create_synced also strips the
  dynamic and CPU-percentage flags before creating its workers.
- ZIO's source table distinguishes issue/completion work, fixed counts and scaling
  floors. Free-issue sizing starts at 32; rounding yields three groups of 11 in
  this environment. Kernel module parameter declarations are not automatically
  a user-space tuning API for static variables in this retained build.
- Reducing reported CPUs alone leaves the fixed 64+4 system workers, file-vdev
  floor of 16 and compile-time ARC sizing. It cannot make this engine fit two
  native child slots or establish a safe global one-worker executor.

The next **experimental service policy** is explicit topology scoping, not a new
native budget: model one assigned CPU, matching the current core-local native
service direction, and compare with the unmodified nine-CPU observation. In a
separate Linux experiment, change only the engine-facing online-CPU query; label
that as a modeled view rather than actual host affinity or native execution.
Keep max_ncpus, physical-memory discovery, stack sizes, queue constants and all
work enabled. Preserve real answers for other queries and prove the modeled
query path is used. Re-run attribution and storage oracles and retain every run.

After that comparison, assess queue dependencies and taskq_wait/synchronization
before any worker-count changes. Keep issue/completion and timer/reclaim progress
paths distinct unless executable evidence establishes a safe alternative. Measure
live concurrency and stack/allocator peaks before selecting reusable admission
or owner-local capacity. Production CPU/resource discovery belongs to the scoped
user-space runtime; a host CPU count must not silently define service authority.
No kernel, engine default, sibling, release pin or native service contract changed
in this attribution experiment. Persistent shell/package integration remains open.

Source review also exposes taskq platform semantics that the small storage passes
must not hide: lib/libzpool/taskq.c taskq_dispatch_delay returns zero without
queuing; taskq_wait_id and taskq_wait_outstanding ignore the ID and drain the whole
queue; successful taskq_dispatch returns a constant 1. Those upstream behaviors
are unchanged here. An ID from this implementation is not a unique operation
identity or a capability, and delayed dispatch is not qualified by this workload.

**Next experiment:** exercise actual taskq execution, cross-queue dependency
progress and draining with explicit bounded queues before reducing worker counts
or sharing an executor. Preserve blocking/completion paths, named failure controls

## Actual taskq dependency and drain result — 2026-09-06

The [taskq witness](evidence/openzfs-taskq-drain-2026-09-06/README.md) executes the
unchanged libzpool taskq implementation with two distinct single-worker fixture
queues. A callback on A dispatches work on B and waits for B; another caller drains
A using taskq_wait_id while a later callback remains queued. Gates prove that
neither active dependency work nor the later callback can be skipped by draining.
Callback TSD identifies the expected queue. A cv_wait observer marks entry into
the real predicate wait and forwards unchanged; it does not replace synchronization.

One warmup plus ten repeated processes pass three fixture cycles each. At the
TQ_NOSLEEP entry limit (A=2, B=1), extra dispatches reject without changing
allocation/list state or executing their callbacks. Empty-state checks and a new
B dispatch prove entry reuse after draining. Both fixture queues are destroyed
and recreated, then the real system queues are finalized. Those system queues
retain their 64+4 defaults; this is not a two-thread process or a native capacity
experiment. Queued-work completion is distinct from kernel thread quiescence:
upstream destroy bookkeeping alone does not prove departing stack/TLS reclamation.

Exact negative controls reject premature drain and false successful delayed
dispatch at exit 2. Actual delayed dispatch returns zero and runs no callback;
cancel returns ENOENT. These results establish the retained implementation's
limitations, not delayed execution or general cancellation support. Constant
successful task IDs remain unsuitable as unique operation identities.

The entry bound is specifically nonblocking: source review of task_alloc shows
TQ_SLEEP may allocate beyond maxalloc after a timed throttle to avoid caller
deadlock. Therefore maxalloc cannot stand in for an Aether memory-grant budget.
That sleeping allocation path is not qualified by this witness. No worker-count,
allocator, upstream, native kernel, sibling or release-pin change was made.

Next: define the minimal native taskq platform bridge and source/symbol boundary
using existing thread/memory grants. Resolve taskq TSD initialization separately
from the fixed system-worker startup, failure behavior for sleeping allocations,
and confirmed native exit/reaping before implementation. Preserve these distinct
progress queues in the first native experiment; the result does not justify a
shared one-worker executor. Live thread/stack/allocator peak measurements remain
required before selecting full-engine service capacity. Filesystem namespace,
Rust service ownership, persistent shell/package integration and release acceptance
remain separate work.

### What the retained failure establishes

The [original UTC intake receipt](evidence/openzfs-shared-utc-2026-10-06.json),
attempt0, names `thread-suite-service-runtime-ru36wxt9`. Its remaining compact
`results.json` hash matches that receipt. The full remaining failed-case serial
log, not merely its eight-line tail, contains admission of slot0/generation1 with
1,316 pages used, four primary clock-park samples, worker scan summary
`scans=2552 max_contexts=86 max_ns=356000 valid=1`, supervisor401/reclaimed0 and
the native-profile panic. It contains no owner terminal or observation rows.
The raw log's memory-map/firmware output does not provide worker PCs or wait state.
`max_contexts=86` is scheduler scan scope, not evidence of86 live application
workers or an exceeded82-worker grant.

The first failing leap engine ELF is
`61aeeb45436ffd3f2c7a62f5e13cdf1a5342c4a6f2abc0ae569418ed2a8abfcf`;
its supervisor is
`c3d1136273b88fb2d8d00b1c933de2f9abc7e00a02177ab1af1c693d8bda6133`.
The preceding normal-date case passes all three owners using that same supervisor,
but a different engine ELF. The initial engine startup, UTC fixture and runner
have recorded hashes different from the later instrumented source. Their original
source bytes and ELF/ISO/map were disposed under the compact-retention decision.
Hashes identify those inputs; they cannot reconstruct an exact binary or schedule.
The retained base supervisor source is byte-identical to today's base, but its
generated variant is not retained. Initial output does not locate the last engine
phase. Later phase telemetry cannot be projected onto this earlier execution.

The case's33.606-second wall interval includes build/boot/check/teardown, so it is
not an engine deadline or measured native deadlock duration. Neither valid clock
scan statistics nor an absent terminal establish calendar correctness, thread
progress, a particular lock owner, exhaustion, clean retirement or device quiescence.
The record supports a bounded missing-terminal failure, not a root cause. No data
transaction or durable acknowledgment was attempted; it is not a lost-commit or
recovery witness. The original UTC receipt reports no qualified disk result for
the failed case, so do not upgrade it to proven media immutability.

### Confirmed defect and scope of the corrected candidate

The [separate ordered reaper witness](evidence/service-runtime-reaper-cycle-2026-10-06.json)
establishes an actual v2 wait cycle: detached B holds the musl list lock while
waiting for reaper permission; the reaper, settling previously stopped A, waits
for that same lock before it can permit B. Exact870/401/zero-reclaim and native
status/lock observations demonstrate this schedule. The sealed UTC-v2 backend
matches the elapsed-v2 backend byte-for-byte. This proves an applicable defect
class in the failing composition, but the original trace never records the lock/
thread states needed to attribute that particular execution to this cycle.

The corrected v3 source removes reaper list-lock acquisition, retains confirmed
native stop before unmap/retire, and publishes the atomic free thread pointer last
after state reset/live accounting. Creators remain serialized by the musl lock.
The [independent corrected intake](evidence/service-runtime-fixed-reaper-2026-10-06.json)
rebuilds exact elapsed/UTC archives and qualifies ordered overlapping exits,
serial retirement,512-admission exhaustion, normal/busy/final finish, stop/
replacement and elapsed source controls. Six UTC cases include normal/leap,
disabled856/policy855,24 actual SPA/taskq reuse cycles and short847 rejection.
The old v2 archive still reaches exact870 with the corrected witness source;
v3 retires normally under its ordered overlap schedule. This is evidence that
the demonstrated defect class was corrected, not exhaustive scheduler coverage.

## Writable-pool preparation contract — 2026-10-07

Before implementation: prepare the smallest native experiment that can establish
writable OpenZFS feasibility without treating a raw write or successful parent ZIO
wait as a durable transaction. This section specifies future acceptance; it does
not authorize valuable media or qualify a pool. The immediate prerequisite is an
actual physical ZIO write/flush witness, followed by pool create/export/import only
after its prerequisites. Earlier UTC missing-terminal attribution and independent
human reader acceptance remain open before persistent acceptance.

Affected owners/profiles: Aether's private C engine/Rust volume adapter and BSP
region-v2 service-memory runner; Aether-userland owns runtime/shared UTC, sys-init
owns activation/recovery policy and pkg-mgr owns installation state. No sibling
change, public SDK/API, default promotion or released-pin change. Relevant rules
are Foundations O-01–05, I-01/03–06/08, E-01/03–08, Distribution DS-01–05 and
reviewed TD/TE/CE/SE clauses.
This is a local experiment contract, not a competing shared filesystem standard.

### Authority, resources and ordering

Use one newly created disposable regular image, one delegated region and one
authoritative engine owner. The native grant alone authorizes access; the fixed
file selector, pool name/GUID and imported config confer no authority. Preserve
denial of replacement generations. A fresh VM's bootstrap may make a fresh grant
only after the prior VM is confirmed gone; close or owner exit is not a regrant
factory. Preserve whole-image outside-region canaries. No real device, host-path
fallback, ambient discovery, concurrent pool writer or package/service activation.

Retain 82 contexts/two service owners, 512 lifetime admissions, 8192 pages per service,
128 regions, the existing native poll bounds and 30-second QEMU watchdog. The
64 MiB region qualified only opening/physical I/O; do not assume it qualifies pool
allocation. Measure create/import heap, helper/reaper, stack and admission costs
within existing bounds. A bound failure is missing feasibility, not permission to
raise quotas or lower upstream cache/metadata floors. Specify the image size and
feature/property closure before a pool run; do not auto-enable all features.

The provider performs synchronous, serialized 512-byte native requests through
private kernel DMA storage. Successful writes report completed sectors, not stable
storage. A multi-sector call is not atomic: a later pointer/authority denial can
follow a confirmed prefix. Preserve exact residual/error and do not retry that
prefix in the adapter. 512-byte transport granularity does not establish atomic
sector writes under interruption or power loss. A real successful native flush is
an ordering boundary for previously completed writes on this one device under the recorded QEMU cache and
host-storage assumptions. It does not prove physical power-loss protection.

Keep original upstream TXG/label ordering. In the pinned 2.4.4 source
`module/zfs/vdev_label.c`, `vdev_config_sync()` flushes prior TXG blocks before
publishing referencing uberblocks; `vdev_uberblock_sync_list()` then flushes the
uberblocks. Do not replace that chain with a marker, successful no-op, host fsync
after the guest stops, or private changes to upstream on-disk state.

### Flush completion and uncertain outcomes

Pinned OpenZFS commit `71a9f9578616a90c3c14bb59629fb4d31bfd68d1` has two important
limits in `module/zfs/zio.c` and `module/zfs/vdev_file.c`: `zio_flush()` issues child
ZIOs with `ZIO_FLAG_DONT_PROPAGATE`, and bypasses a leaf marked `vdev_nowritecache`;
the file-vdev also bypasses fsync when `zfs_nocacheflush` is set. Later
`zio_vdev_io_done()` converts flush ENOTSUP into success and sets
`vdev_nowritecache`, so inspect the provider error before that conversion too.
Therefore a zero parent wait alone cannot be the flush oracle. Before acceptance,
require those bypasses disabled, a concrete readable leaf, actual file-vdev fsync dispatch and
matching provider/native completion with zero error. A missing/error completion
must prevent the experiment's durability acknowledgment even if the parent returns
zero. Do not silently claim a general public sync contract from this private gate.

| Observed outcome | Required treatment |
| --- | --- |
| Local validation/rights/stale-grant rejection before issue | Report the exact cause and zero issued effects; previous completed sectors remain effects. |
| Confirmed sector prefix, later rejection | Report that prefix and error; no implicit rollback, full success or adapter replay. |
| Completed writes, no successful flush | Written data may be visible; no durable acknowledgment. |
| Missing terminal/callback or flush observation | Outcome remains unknown; timeout does not establish no writes or quiescence. |
| Issued device timeout/invalid completion/I/O failure | Current block driver panics and retains DMA; no recoverable errno, live replacement, cancellation or automatic replay. |
| VM teardown confirmed after interrupted run | Fresh VM may inspect the same owned media with fresh authority; validate upstream labels/transactions and data, never replay an unacknowledged application operation by assumption. |

Borrowed C file/ABD/request/buffer and Rust metadata remain valid and serialized
through actual completion. Drain upstream helpers and outstanding ZIOs before
freeing their references; clean export precedes engine/runtime retirement. An
unclean run makes no clean-reclaim claim. Current device quiescence for uncertain
issued work is confirmed VM process teardown, not owner stop/revoke. Live service
restart after uncertain device work stays unavailable until a separate device
quiescence/recovery contract is implemented and qualified.

### Executable sequence and remaining gates

1. Exercise original physical write ZIO, actual leaf/provider flush and readback
   on raw disposable media, then read the same media in a fresh VM. Check exact
   callback identity/count/error, ABD guards, full-image canaries, clean helper
   retirement and replacement denial. Include missing write/flush completion,
   flush-rights/stale-grant denials and a deliberately suppressed flush. A parent
   wait with no provider completion must fail the oracle. No pool claim follows.
2. Resolve the existing UTC attribution gate and obtain independent reader
   acceptance. Audit the newly linked create/import/TXG dependency closure. Use
   explicit pool properties including `cachefile=none`; the provider intentionally
   cannot create/write a host cache file. Import uses bounded inspected config and
   the same explicit selector, with no device scan, force-import or automatic rewind.
3. Within measured bounds, run original pool create and a single committed DMU
   object with fixed identity/data; clean export, VM teardown, fresh native import
   and checksum/data verification. A DMU object is not a mounted POSIX filesystem.
   Freeze the selected features, config/artifact identity and flush witness before
   considering this a persistence checkpoint; run full affected integration suites
   and the broad host checkpoint there.
4. Before adopting the port for application/package data, exercise interruption
   before/after the acknowledged commit, recovery of the last acknowledged data,
   ENOSPC and malformed metadata. No interrupted operation receives fabricated
   success; corruption must reject explicitly. Verify recovery without automatic
   replay, silent destructive rewind or widening authority. Retain compact failed
   attempts, never image/build archives.

## Storage connection admission contract — 2026-10-18

Requirement: give the original-engine worker an authenticated request/reply
connection before it can execute. This is a prerequisite for publishing Rust
filesystem handles, not a general filesystem or released SDK ABI. Existing
`AENBD002`/ABI14 storage and launch profiles remain unchanged. The separate opt-in
`native-service-channel` selects `AENBD003`/ABI25 and requires service block,
workers and entropy. It retains the two worker slots, three lifetimes, 32-byte
queues, 16-byte transfers, memory budgets and READ-only fixture grant.

The supervisor explicitly creates two empty channels with its existing `0xd001`
factory. New profile-only operation `0x20008` takes factory `RDI=0xa001`, worker
slot `RSI`, request reader `RDX`, reply writer `R10`, and zero `R8/R9`. The caller
must own **both ends** of both distinct, live, empty channels. Direction, ownership,
queue state, slot, lifetime and image budget all validate before any worker or
endpoint mutation. Failure preserves generations, mappings and every endpoint.
Success transfers only those two ends to the child, supplies them in auxv
`0xa004/0xa005`, returns the child handle, and makes the child runnable only after
binding. Legacy unconnected creation is unavailable in this profile. There is no
worker channel factory and no caller-selected sender identity or authority table.

Kernel endpoint lookup authenticates the invoking domain on every transfer.
The service must select its peer/session from its retained request endpoint,
never request bytes; a client may supply object tokens but cannot attach itself.
Within this bounded boot profile the nonreused request endpoint identity provides
a provider epoch. Tokens are boot-local and must not survive a reboot. General
service provisioning or reuse beyond this profile needs its own epoch contract.
Worker terminal/stop closes its ends before terminal publication. The supervisor
must drain/close its remaining ends before reuse; final close zeroes unread data.

Forbidden outcomes include wrong-direction or foreign endpoint transfer, launch
after failed preflight, reusing a live channel, stale-token revival, receiving a
request before binding, and claiming that a synthetic peer label authenticates
anything. The fixed supervisor remains a conformance fixture; sys-init lifecycle,
userland descriptor policy and package policy stay with their owners.

Implementation/test selection: shared channel preflight tests must cover each
denial and unchanged state, transfer/closure and three-generation exhaustion;
compile the affected kernel profile and exercise actual user-domain admission,
partial transfers, foreign/stale capabilities and cleanup before filesystem
publication. Focused Rust owner/provider checks remain relevant. A full host
checkpoint and matching native integration are required before claiming the
composed service works. New unsafe assembly must state register, buffer and
completion ownership; no archive retention or native retries on uncertain outcomes.

Simplicity: using the existing channels and supplying their ends during admission
is smaller than a second transport, kernel filesystem RPC or post-launch binding
protocol. Plain unconnected admission is insufficient because it races execution
with delegation. This adds one private opt-in admission operation and no new
mutable authority store; no existing consumed profile is removed. Kernel code
only admits/mediates channels; filesystem framing and policy remain in Rust.

### Private readonly request framing

One connection, provisioned with LOOKUP/READ authority, first receives a 64-byte
root reply. All words below are little-endian u64. Each request/reply is exactly
64 bytes, assembled over the existing partial byte transfers. Requests have
opcode at word0, opaque handle epoch/serial at words1/2, arguments at words3/4,
and 24 payload bytes at offset40. There is no peer/session/attach field.

| Opcode | Arguments and payload | Successful reply |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 lookup | word3 requested rights1/2/3; word4 name length1–24; single relative name then zero padding | Opaque handle words1/2, kind word3 (directory1/file2), size word4 |
| 2 read | word3 offset; word4 length0–24; zero payload | Count word4, exact data at40, all other bytes zero |
| 3 close | Zero words3/4 and payload | All zero |
| 4 disconnect | Zero words1–4 and payload | All zero, connection revoked before acknowledgment |

Reply word0 is signed status:0 on success, negative error on failure; every other
byte in an error reply is zero. Provider errors become EIO rather than publishing
raw engine details. Unsupported opcodes, noncanonical padding and oversized
requests reject; lookup/path/type/rights/epoch checks remain with the Rust owner.
Request tokens are untrusted data and gain no authority from decoding. No raw
backend identity, pointer, UID, host path or ambient attach operation is exposed.

The synchronous adapter uses initialized exclusive frame buffers, copies no more
than 16 bytes per call and yields on EAGAIN. Partial EOF, errors, impossible byte
counts and 100000-call exhaustion clear the buffer and retire the local connection;
they never dispatch a partial request. The server bounds one session to 64 requests.
All completion paths revoke handles and close the original C context before SPA
drain/retirement. This is a private bounded experiment, not general framing,
asynchronous cancellation, a public filesystem SDK or credential/path policy.

The opt-in native fixture retains the whole-file original-C SHA check, then opens
one additional owned context for actual separate-domain lookup/read/close/disconnect.
It verifies a 16-byte read's ELF identification prefix and EOF, rights and
stale-close denials. Directory/forged-epoch/unknown-opcode checks remain in the
owner/wire tests and earlier component qualification. This does not claim that the entire
file was transferred over IPC. Replacement workers retain storage denial and
exercise endpoint terminal closure/zeroing/reuse through all three lifetimes.
The client is size-optimized with fat LTO in this profile only; the unchanged
4096-byte/no-writable-static supervisor linker assertions remain enforcing.

Preparation and native commands (both require the explicit sealed v3 deliveries):

```sh
python3 -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_namespace_ipc.py -v
zig test kernel/native_channel.zig --cache-dir .zig-cache --global-cache-dir .zig-cache/global
python3 scripts/service_runtime_smoke.py --fixed-reaper --engine-utc --pool-create --pool-namespace --namespace-ipc --pool-diagnose build --case pool-import --delivery ../Aether-userland/build/sdk-deliveries/native-service-runtime-2026-10-06-v3 --utc-delivery ../Aether-userland/build/sdk-deliveries/native-utc-2026-10-06-v3
```

After preparation/freeze, replace `build` with `run` for one declared READ-only
guest. Preserve unexpected outcomes and stop under the accepted recurrence rule;
no automatic native retry. Existing namespace command without `--namespace-ipc`
retains its earlier component profile and does not publish a connection.

## Private ZPL write prerequisite contract — 2026-10-18

Requirement before implementation: prepare bounded writes to an **existing**
regular ZPL5/SA file through original DMU/SA transactions. This precedes file
creation and writable Rust publication. No active build, native profile, wire
opcode, WRITE grant, SDK, kernel, pin or sibling changes in this slice. The private
writer translation unit includes the existing reader implementation and replaces
that unit when eventually selected; it must never be linked alongside it. A small
internal open-mode helper shares validation without duplicating disk-format code.
The exported readonly factory retains its exact mode/ownership contract.

Source review found that original writable `dmu_objset_own` can dispatch accounting
upgrades. The factory therefore first validates under **readonly DMU ownership on
the writable SPA**. Check readonly property, snapshot and accounting completeness
before any writable own; reject user-object/project/user accounting upgrades.
Close the entire probe, drop its borrowed SA mapping and reacquire/revalidate a new
writable context. Dataset/property changes are excluded across that transition.
Completed accounting may still dispatch an original no-op helper; native resource/
drain qualification must include it. No implicit accounting migration or feature
activation is selected by this private writer. Faulting doubles count writable-own
attempts and must detect guards removed before the transition.

The trusted engine owner supplies a live writable SPA borrow, configured dataset,
validated object/generation, initialized input buffer and explicit UTC seconds/
nanoseconds established by Aether-userland policy. These are private owner inputs,
not client authority. The new factory requires writable SPA, a non-snapshot objset
owned for writing, and readonly property0; full existing format/root validation
still applies. Context calls, dataset/property mutation, import/export and teardown
are serialized. No pointer escapes; close consumes the context before SPA retirement.

Write validates nonzero matching generation, linked regular SA inode, existing
size/timestamp/flags attributes and bounded input before assignment. Reject
set-ID files and immutable/append-only/readonly/xattr flags rather than inventing
credential or append policy. Accept overwrite or append with no gap, length0–4096,
end at most INT64_MAX, seconds0–INT64_MAX and nanoseconds below1e9. Copy input before
assignment; zero-length work creates no transaction. Existing allocation/feature/
media constraints remain; no rangelock/concurrent writer/ZIL/atime/ACL policy is
claimed. Original APIs own DMU allocation/decoding and SA updates; no custom disk
format or DMU_OST_OTHER filesystem.

One transaction holds both data and existing SA attributes, assigns with WAIT,
captures its TXG, writes data and updates size/mtime/ctime/archive flags. Assignment
failure aborts and reports no effects. Every assigned transaction commits even
when metadata update fails. The result has three u64 words: TXG, bytes staged and
state (0 unassigned/no-op,1 committed,2 synced). It is initialized before validation.
Post-assignment error preserves committed/staged progress; it never fabricates an
untouched result, retries, aborts assigned work or returns a success byte count.
Only successful metadata update proceeds to original wait-synced, and state2
requires an observed last-synced TXG at least the assigned target. A zero target,
failed metadata update/wait or early wait is failure requiring caller retirement
and investigation; no further mutation may use that context after such failure.

State2 is **not a public durability acknowledgment**. Before native selection,
join the actual assigned transaction to the existing ordered root/child/device
completion witness, qualify resource/stack fit and fresh-VM readback/recovery, then
define Rust WRITE authority and conservative failure/poison behavior. The readonly
owner caches size per handle: writable publication must also resolve metadata
coherency across aliases, not update just one handle's cached size. Creation must
use valid upstream ZPL metadata and remains a separate required operation.

Tests: actual C over faulting API doubles must check write/SA/commit/wait order,
every pre/post-assignment error, exact progress, zero/overflow/type/flags/time/
readonly/stale denials, balanced ownership and compiling semantic omissions.
Compile against pinned original native headers with exact import/dependency/tool
identities and stack usage. Compare the readonly object before/after the shared
factory extraction, and run the affected reader/owner/C-ABI tests. No full suite
or guest repeat is selected for an unselected private helper; broaden at native
writable integration or if unchanged-profile identity/behavior cannot be preserved.
Retain compact results and failed attempts; dispose generated outputs.

Simplicity: a synchronous original-API helper with explicit progress is smaller
than premature writable RPC, a new transaction service or a generic commit API.
Its extra result state is necessary because post-assignment errors cannot mean
"nothing happened." Shared validation avoids a second ZPL decoder/open path.
No consumed experiment can be retired yet. Aether owns the engine boundary;
userland keeps UTC/POSIX policy, sys-init lifecycle and pkg-mgr installation policy.
Borrowed API sequencing is reviewed against the pinned original zfs_write and
timestamp logic; no upstream function body is transplanted or relicensed.

## Namespace service implementation contract — 2026-10-19

Requirement: carry the existing bounded original-engine create/rename/remove
operations through authenticated Rust service policy, the next concrete UL-082
step toward persistent Zsh/packages. This is a separately selected private
`fs_namespace` build, mutually exclusive with `fs_lifetimes`; old sealed deliveries,

mutation attempt per engine (shared with the old nonempty write budget), no
observer retirement/reuse/reset, no transaction/memory/time/channel increase.
The existing original C mutator serializes a live writable/accounted SPA/context.

Authority: add explicit MUTATE_DIR8 only to the new profile, distinct from
file WRITE4. Require both LOOKUP1 and MUTATE_DIR8 on an authenticated directory
handle before namespace effects. UID/GID are bounded trusted bootstrap labels,
not request fields or authority; no default ambient identity. Resolve victim IDs
inside the provider after checked leaf names. Remove rejects any live file alias
in any connection before dispatch (no open-unlink); create is empty0600 regular,
rename same-directory/no replace, remove single-link/no external xattr/<=1MiB.
No directories, links, replacement, cross-directory move, ACL inheritance or
arbitrary POSIX semantics. Keep native object/generation identities out of client
replies. Old profiles reject bit8 and the new wire/version.

Private AFSRPC02 keeps64-byte initialized frames and16-byte payload,64 complete
requests/bounded polling. Opcodes1..6 retain their field layouts. New7=create,
8=rename,9=remove: word3 directory token; word4 zero; word5 source-name length in
low32 bits and destination length in high32 (rename only); bytes48..63 contain
source then destination, with zero tail. Names each1..16 bytes; combined<=16;
reject empty/dot/dotdot/NUL/slash and all unused bits/fields. No raw victim IDs,
credentials or client clock. Success returns only assigned TXG in word4,0 staged
in word5,state2 in word6. No newly allocated client handle is published.

One actual namespace provider attempt permanently retires all policy aliases and
consumes the C context before one response attempt, then both endpoint closes.
A clean transferred success ends with explicit stop6/namespace-complete; this is
not disconnect/readiness/durability. Known unassigned errors also consume the
attempt and terminate; committed errors retain exact state1/TXG (including0),0
staged and positive errno. Malformed/lost progress becomes unknown/retired, never
replayed. Denied/malformed/preflight failures do not mutate or spend the attempt.
C close must consume/null; owned factory error outputs close exactly once.
Never pass zero staged bytes to the16-byte or4096-byte retirement entry. The new
trusted `aether_fs_namespace_run` entry adds explicit UID/GID bootstrap inputs and
keeps the72-byte result, with no export of the old entry in this build.

## Proposed mixed-operation service lifetime gate — 2026-10-19

Maintainer approved this exact fixed mixed profile on2026-10-19. Implementation
and matched native qualification may proceed within these limits; no additional
approval is needed for this same scope. Existing qualified profiles remain unchanged.
The one-attempt namespace gate consumes its context/aliases before replying. It
cannot become a continuous service by resetting that flag or passing zero staged
bytes to the qualified16-byte retirement entry. DEVELOPMENT's approved eight-write
exception is exactly eight16-byte writes, not eight mixed namespace operations.
That separate decision is now recorded; it does not generalize the earlier profile.

Proposed separate private variant: exactly eight total provider mutation attempts,
not eight per kind, one authenticated connection/64 complete requests/16-byte data
payload. Fixed sequence on the existing pristine dataset:

1. Create `p` as an empty0600 regular file with trusted UID/GID1000.
2. Lookup and write16 bytes at0.
3. Write16 bytes at16, extending it to32.
4. Rename `p` to `q`; retain and check the existing file handle.
5. Write16 bytes at32 through that same handle, extending it to48.
6. Close every file alias, then remove `q` using a provider-resolved identity.
7. Create `p` again with a fresh identity; reject the retired old handle.
8. Lookup/write16 bytes at0; verify the exact final file, `q` absence and original
   untouched780248-byte zstd file independently, then fresh-VM recovery.

At most four namespace attempts and four16-byte writes. A ninth dispatch rejects;
never reset its shared counter on close/reopen. Any actual failed/unknown outcome,
retirement/reopen/root-refresh error or lost reply retires permanently without
replay. Existing single-attempt namespace, one-write/eight-write and4096 profiles,
sealed candidates and released/SDK pins remain unchanged. Introduce an explicit
private variant/entry and AFSRPC03 readiness so consumers cannot infer continuing
semantics from AFSRPC02 stop6. Normal completion uses explicit disconnect, with
no implicit service-ready/durability/restart claim.

Require a separately typed success-only namespace0 retirement boundary, distinct
from service16/4096. It must consume/null the original C context before checking
original error0/state2/nonzero assigned TXG/staged0, all original graph/pins/
requests/operations/wait/epoch checks, and retain permanent failures, first target,
cumulative completion and device identity. No arbitrary-length retirement API,
failed-record reset or client-controlled retirement. Reopen only after checked
retirement; validate the original root identity and refresh directory aliases
before another request. Keep valid file aliases across rename, update all file
sizes on writes, and deny remove with any live alias. The fixture is explicitly
restricted to its provisioned dataset root; this does not invent general directory
cache coherence. Unknown/stale metadata retires the whole owner.

Trusted directory bootstrap would select explicit rights15 (LOOKUP1, READ2,
WRITE4, MUTATE_DIR8); file capabilities cannot contain MUTATE_DIR. This changes
the selected fixture's rights9, not the kernel rights model or default grant.
No raw victim IDs, credentials, clock or pointers in client frames. Keep serialized
exclusive SPA/provider ownership and userland UTC policy; sys-init/pkg-mgr roles
remain activation/installation. No sibling-owner acceptance is assumed.

All other ceilings stay fixed: writer60s/6000, readers30s/3000, controller4096/no
writable statics, combined observer8192, backing8192 pages, two channels/three
generations/32 observations and all worker/I/O/authority limits. If it does not
fit, stop rather than increase them. Before any guest, qualify exact0-versus16/
4096 staging rejection, borrowed graph/request/pin and old-epoch/failure/overflow
controls, alias/cache coherence, shared exhaustion, partial/lost replies and close
errors; compile semantic omissions and all affected old variants. Then prepare
and admit the complete matched normal/lost-first-reply/final-partial-input/final-
close-failure matrix before launch, preserving independent full original/new file,
namespace/metadata/ACL/allocation/EOF/fresh recovery/media/export/drain/cleanup
oracles. Failure stops for triage; no automatic retry. Compact evidence and disposal
remain mandatory. This is the next bounded lifetime gate, not operational idle,
large-file transport, executable/init assembly or persistent Zsh ISO acceptance.

Simplicity: resetting the existing one-attempt owner would lose outcome/alias
invariants; the existing consumed-context success-only mechanism is the smaller

using the established observer rather than adding a journal or service boundary.
No qualified old path can yet be removed. Full operational interface and its
