## Operational service handoff after the mixed gate — 2026-10-19

Updated2026-10-20: current deliveries include the bridge and finite25/27/36/41/
43/49-member candidates. Namespace RPCs, typed0/16 retirement and writable ABI26
primary waits have bounded producer evidence. The approved AF04 data candidate
adds length1..4096 and checked retirement; its native gate is tracked in STATE.
These results do not turn a relocatable candidate into a boot service. The next
UL-082 handoff needs a coherent contract/delivery for these R1 requirements:

- Exact service executable, entry/auxv/runtime/kernel ABI, retained engine/SPA/
  dataset ownership and enforcing block/channel grants. No ambient disk/path or
  global privileged shortcut. One owner serializes mutable engine state.
- Operational idle and wake behavior with close/EOF/stop/notification races.
  The separate AENBD004/ABI26 storage profile now qualifies primary wait0x24004
  with helpers schedulable. Reuse that mechanism; its bounded workload does not
  establish service uptime, activation readiness or general shutdown semantics.
  Retain qualified old profiles and pins until consuming owners migrate.
- Actual shell/runner/package reads and writes, their buffer/handle lifetimes,
  partial/error/uncertain outcomes and required namespace operations. The current
  AF04 data ceiling is4096 bytes per request; names remain16 inline bytes and
  namespace mutations remain root-only. The64-request/eight-attempt limit is
  explicit; package/executable transfer or an ongoing shell cannot silently
  bypass it. Required directories, layout and metadata policy also need an
  explicit contract; data transport alone does not supply them. Propose replacement
  limits/workloads with resource/authority/verification implications before code;
  obtain maintainer decisions for any changed explicit experiment ceiling.
- Provisioning/ready/shutdown/drain semantics that sys-init can consume, with
  installation/trust policy still owned by pkg-mgr and POSIX/UTC policy by
  Aether-userland. Ready must distinguish an imported usable filesystem from a
  linked object or a test marker. Preserve original engine export/drain ownership
  and retained terminal/unknown outcomes; no automatic mutation replay.

Aether-userland owns that operational contract; Aether owns its matched native
engine/profile integration. Existing authorization permits scoped userland work,
not inferred changes to unrelated sys-init/pkg-mgr/standards repositories. Compose
one smallest executable persistent workload, then init/runner/packages/Zsh and
VM boot media; a RAM-only or bounded test-shell image does not meet the maintainer's
requested ISO. A fixed transaction matrix is not sustained uptime, crash/power-
loss assurance, whole-stack/peak-memory proof, independent owner/reader acceptance
or R1 readiness. Preserve existing sources/pins/candidates until a qualified
replacement identifies actual consumers and recovery implications.

## Primary storage-channel wait contract — 2026-10-20

Next requirement: remove active polling as a prerequisite to an operational
filesystem service, without suspending the storage domain's runtime helpers.

## Proposed next data gate: 4 KiB service payloads — 2026-10-20

Status: **maintainer-approved2026-10-20; qualified for the bounded native data matrix**. The matched
ABI26 mixed-wait qualification now passes its bounded native matrix. The remaining16-byte service payload is
insufficient for shell executable/package transfer. The previous explicit
maintainer decision covers16-byte writes; changing that experiment ceiling needs
its own decision under DEVELOPMENT. This proposal changes only a separately
selected private data profile; it does not promote an operational ABI or ISO.

Proposed contract for maintainer decision:

- New AFSRPC04 candidate and distinct trusted entry, exact closed intake, existing
  ABI26/scalar holder waits. Preserve all old AFSRPC01–03 candidates/profiles and
  SDK/released pins. Userland owns Rust policy/framing; Aether owns exact engine/
  block/native composition. Sys-init/package/UTC ownership remains unchanged.
- Keep64-byte headers and the existing64-byte frame transport. Namespace names
  remain inline within the existing16-byte name area. Read/write lengths may be
  1..4096; their header's old inline-data area must be zero. A write carries exactly
  ceil(length/64) following data frames; a successful read response carries that
  many frames for its actual count. Last-frame unused bytes must be zero. Error
  responses have no data body. No new kernel opcode, shared-memory grant, variable
  unbounded frame, request multiplexing or resynchronization protocol.
- Validate header/rights/token/offset/length before provider effects. Fully receive
  and validate a write body before dispatch. Invalid data-bearing headers, unknown
  opcodes or partial bodies end the connection after at most one error response;
  never parse their tail as another command. Existing known namespace/header-only
  denials can return canonical errors and continue. Clear initialized body/header
  storage on failure. A partial read reply is never replayed; original write errno/
  TXG/staged/state remain visible after committed/unknown outcomes.
- One reusable4096-byte service buffer, borrowed exclusively by synchronous C I/O;
  no kernel pointer survives EAGAIN/wait. Stream client test data through existing
  64-byte frames rather than adding a4096-byte controller buffer. Check actual
  frames/code/static/stack implications before guests; fit the existing mappings
  and controller limit or stop. Typed success-only retirement accepts namespace0 or a write length1..4096
  bound to the validated request, equal to the complete original staged count;
  the native positive workload always writes4096. A wrong expected count, partial/
  unknown outcome, failed record, live pin/graph or bad epoch must refuse reuse.
  This length-checked trusted retirement is needed for real package tails, not an
  arbitrary record-reset API. Old0/16 wrappers remain exact. No failed reset/replay.
- Fixed positive sequence stays8 total mutations: create p; writes4096 at0 and4096;
  rename p to q retaining the file handle; write4096 at8192; close every file alias
  and remove q; recreate p/fresh identity; write4096 at0. Each byte is its existing
  seed11/31/51/71 plus offset modulo256. Final p is4096 bytes; q absent; original
  780248-byte zstd unchanged. Rights15/trusted UID/GID1000/root-only alias policy,
  no ninth dispatch and64 complete-request cap stay fixed. Body frames are part
  of a request, not independently dispatchable commands. Existing100000 transfer
  attempts apply per64-byte frame, including spurious readiness rescans; a4096-byte
  body therefore has at most64 bounded frames. The outer deadlines remain mandatory.
- Keep the four-case fresh-media matrix. Normal/final-close-error cases complete
  the fixed8 operations. Lost-first-create-reply stops after create, with empty p.
  Partial-body case sends only a16-byte prefix of the first4096-byte write body,
  after create/lookup; no write dispatch, empty p, original create outcome retained.
  Prepare/admit all distinct images before guests; use fresh independent readers
  for complete content/hash, size/mode/identity/ACL/time/EOF, namespace/allocation,
  original file, media/guards and original export/drain/all cleanup. Do not infer
  success from a greeting, local alias read or numeric observation alone.
- Writers remain60s/6000, readers30s/3000; observer8192, controller4096/no writable
  statics, backing8192 pages, two channels/three generations/32 observations and
  existing worker/I/O/authority limits stay fixed. No automatic retry or workload/
  bound relaxation after failure. Host tests cover lengths1/63/64/65/4095/4096,
  reject4097/overflow/stale/rights/padding before effects, every body-frame boundary,
  read/write backpressure/spurious/wait errors, partial/lost output, original
  outcomes/aliases/budget and cleanup; compiling semantic omissions must fail.

Simplicity: enlarging inline frames would duplicate transport machinery and
increase controller buffers. A bounded sequence of existing frames composes with
qualified readiness and the backend's existing4096-byte limit; one serialized
connection needs no IDs, queues or shared-buffer lease protocol. It removes no
qualified compatibility path now. This is the next necessary data-path gate,
not sufficient operational service acceptance: executable/provisioning, service
lifetime, readiness/shutdown, directory/layout and POSIX/runner/package/Zsh
composition remain before the requested persistent ISO. No independent sign-off,
whole-stack/peak-memory, crash/power-loss or R1 acceptance is implied.
